# vid2sub M1: the CLI, end to end — Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development
> (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use
> checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** an installable Python CLI, `vid2sub`, that turns a local video or a YouTube URL into
SRT/ASS subtitles next to the video, in the video's language and translated into English, using
the DGX Spark or an in-process model on the Mac, with an append-only sidecar.

**Architecture:** a core library (`vid2sub.subs`, `vid2sub.parse` and `vid2sub.catalog` are pure
and will later run in the browser; `vid2sub.media`, `vid2sub.engines`, `vid2sub.translate` and
`vid2sub.store` do the I/O) with an argparse CLI on top. Transcription engines return one
normalised word shape; vid2sub builds the cues itself; translation goes through chatlas; every file
write goes through one safe writer, and every network client through one factory.

**Tech stack:** Python ≥3.12, uv, hatchling, pydantic 2, pysubs2, opencc-purepy,
charset-normalizer, numpy, onnxruntime (Silero VAD v6, vendored from faster-whisper), openai (for
`/v1/audio/transcriptions`), chatlas (translation), httpx, yt-dlp + deno, ffmpeg/ffprobe,
`mlx-qwen3-asr` (Mac extra), pytest, hypothesis, jsonschema, ruff.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-vid2sub-design.md`. This plan implements milestone M1
(spec §25). M2 (editor), M3 (add/review, every platform) and M4 (Pages, releases) get their own
plans.

## Decisions made while planning (review these)

1. **This plan is M1 only.** Each later milestone gets its own plan, as `recordings` does per
   stage.
2. **No `serve` command and no web code in M1.** `vid2sub serve` arrives with M2.
3. **`faster-whisper` is M3** (spec §25). M1's engines are the endpoint engine (whisper.cpp and
   OpenAI response shapes) and `mlx-qwen3-asr` in-process. The `mlx-qwen3-asr serve` response
   parser is deferred: its `verbose_json` shape isn't documented, and the in-process engine covers
   the Mac.
4. **Head records carry `replaces`** (the head rendition they replaced). This refines spec §14.2
   so a conflict is "more than one head that nothing replaced", and a deliberate switch to a fresh
   rendition isn't mistaken for one.
5. **`vid2sub run` keeps the machine awake** while it works. The spec puts sleep inhibition in M3
   with the queue, but a season run from the CLI needs it now.
6. **ASS rewrites keep the existing file's Script Info, Styles and every event not in vid2sub's
   `Default` style.** That is M1's form of spec §15.2's "events vid2sub can't match".
7. **Cantonese tracks are Traditional only in v1** (`yue-Hant`, file code `zh-HK`); `--script`
   applies to Mandarin.
8. **Translation batches run one at a time** in M1, whatever a model's `concurrency` says.
9. **The CLI accepts relative paths**, resolved against the current directory. The web UI's
   fully-qualified-path rule (spec §6.5) arrives with the UI in M2.
10. **`vid2sub cache clear` is not in M1:** nothing is cached until M3's preview copies.
11. **Transcription calls and chat calls go through wrappers in `engines/clients.py`,** which is
    the only module allowed to import `openai`, `httpx` or `chatlas`. That is how the one-factory
    rule (spec §6.4) is enforced by a test.

## Global Constraints

- Python `>=3.12`; CI runs 3.12 and 3.14.
- Pure packages (`vid2sub.subs`, `vid2sub.parse`, `vid2sub.catalog`) import only the standard
  library (no subprocess, socket, http, urllib, asyncio, threading, multiprocessing, ssl),
  pydantic, pysubs2, opencc_purepy, charset_normalizer and each other. pydantic floor 2.10.5
  (Pyodide's).
- Times are integer milliseconds everywhere, except recordings' transcript payload (seconds, 3
  decimals).
- Never log or print to `--json` output: titles, file paths, tokens, keys (spec §6.8). Titles may
  reach an interactive terminal only.
- `vid2sub.toml` holds only env var *names* for keys (`key_env`); a key-looking value is refused.
- Only `src/vid2sub/engines/clients.py` imports `openai`, `httpx` or `chatlas`.
- A cloud model is used only when the run sets `allow_cloud` (`--allow-cloud`); nothing presets it.
- Every file vid2sub writes goes through `vid2sub.security.safe_write`. A file with no projection
  record in the sidecar is never overwritten without `--overwrite-foreign`.
- Every subprocess goes through `vid2sub.media.tools.run_tool`: absolute executable, argument
  list, `cwd` = scratch, `child_env()`, timeout.
- Uploads are named `chunk-NNNN.flac` and carry no metadata.
- Exit codes: 0 ok, 1 an input failed, 64 usage, 69 unavailable, 75 temporary, 78 configuration.
- Test names are sentences. A guard test must fail without its fix: after it passes, revert the
  fix once, watch it fail, restore it.
- British spelling in docs, comments and messages. Module docstrings: one sentence of purpose, then
  why, citing spec sections.
- Before using a library, check its current docs (CLAUDE.md's table); say what you checked in the
  commit body.
- Commits: `type(scope): plain behavioural sentence`, a body saying what was checked, ending with
  `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`.
- Run everything through uv: `uv run pytest`, `uv run ruff check`. Never `pip install`.

## Review Focus

1. **An MKV whose audio starts after its video** (an audio delay): cues must line up with what
   you hear. → Task 19 (offset from the probe) and Task 31 (the pipeline adds it), with a
   generated clip.
2. **A folder that already has `Show.en.srt` from Bazarr:** vid2sub must not overwrite it, and
   `--skip-existing` must skip that target. → Task 28 (foreign files) and Task 31.
3. **Titles and names with characters Windows rejects** (`a/b: c?`, `CON`, bidi marks), from
   YouTube or a file: written names stay safe. → Task 16 (`safe_component`) and Task 24.
4. **Continuous speech longer than a chunk, or a word on a chunk boundary:** no word lost or
   duplicated. → Task 23, a property test over chunk ownership.
5. **Ctrl-C or a dropped tunnel halfway through a season:** re-running with `--skip-existing`
   resumes, and a half-written sidecar line doesn't break anything. → Task 9 (truncated last
   line), Task 28 (compare-and-swap) and Task 32 (exit 75, resume).

```toml
[project]
name = "vid2sub"
version = "0.1.0.dev0"
description = "Subtitles for videos and YouTube, made on your own machine"
readme = "README.md"
license = "MIT"
requires-python = ">=3.12"
authors = [{ name = "Daniel Chen" }]
dependencies = [
    "pydantic>=2.10",
    "pysubs2>=1.9",
    "opencc-purepy>=1.4.4",
    "charset-normalizer>=3.4",
    "platformdirs>=4.3",
    "numpy>=2.0",
    "onnxruntime>=1.20",
    "chatlas>=0.23",
    "openai>=1.100",
    "httpx>=0.27",
    "yt-dlp[default]>=2026.1.1",
]

[project.optional-dependencies]
# Qwen3-ASR with its forced aligner on Apple Silicon (spec §9.4). macOS 14+ only.
mac = ["mlx-qwen3-asr[aligner]>=0.4.4; sys_platform == 'darwin' and platform_machine == 'arm64'"]

[project.scripts]
vid2sub = "vid2sub.cli:main"

# A dependency group, not an extra, so `uv sync` can never silently remove pytest
# (an audio-router lesson).
[dependency-groups]
dev = ["pytest>=8.3", "hypothesis>=6.100", "jsonschema>=4.23", "ruff>=0.8"]

[build-system]
requires = ["hatchling>=1.27"]
build-backend = "hatchling.build"

[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel]
packages = ["src/vid2sub"]

[tool.pytest.ini_options]
testpaths = ["tests"]
pythonpath = ["tests"]  # tests/helpers.py and tests/fakes.py are importable
addopts = "--import-mode=importlib -m 'not live'"
markers = [
    "media: needs ffmpeg and ffprobe (skipped without them unless VID2SUB_REQUIRE_FFMPEG=1)",
    "live: a real model or the Spark; opt-in, see tests/README.md",
]

[tool.ruff]
line-length = 100
target-version = "py312"
```

`src/vid2sub/exitcodes.py`:
```python
"""Exit codes, after sysexits, as in audio-router (spec §18.2)."""

OK = 0
FAILED = 1  # at least one input failed
USAGE = 64
UNAVAILABLE = 69  # a tool, model or endpoint isn't there
TEMPFAIL = 75  # interrupted, or a connection dropped: re-run to carry on
CONFIG = 78
```

`src/vid2sub/commands/__init__.py`:
```python
"""One module per CLI command, each with `add_parser(subparsers)` (spec §18.1)."""
```

`src/vid2sub/cli.py`:
```python
"""vid2sub's command line (spec §18).

vid2sub is a CLI application in its own right: everything that makes, translates or writes
subtitles is a command here, and the web app (M2) calls the same core. `vid2sub` with no
arguments prints help. Each command is a module in `vid2sub.commands` whose `add_parser`
registers it and sets `handler`. Usage errors exit 64, not argparse's 2 (sysexits, §18.2).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import importlib
import sys
from collections.abc import Sequence

from vid2sub import __version__, exitcodes

COMMAND_MODULES: list[str] = []


class _Parser(argparse.ArgumentParser):
    def error(self, message: str) -> None:  # type: ignore[override]
        self.print_usage(sys.stderr)
        self.exit(exitcodes.USAGE, f"{self.prog}: error: {message}\n")


def build_parser() -> argparse.ArgumentParser:
    parser = _Parser(
        prog="vid2sub",
        description="Subtitles for videos and YouTube, made on your own machine.",
    )
    parser.add_argument("--version", action="version", version=f"vid2sub {__version__}")
    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", metavar="COMMAND", parser_class=_Parser)
    for name in COMMAND_MODULES:
        importlib.import_module(f"vid2sub.commands.{name}").add_parser(sub)
    return parser


def main(argv: Sequence[str] | None = None) -> int:
    parser = build_parser()
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    if args.command is None:
        parser.print_help()
        return exitcodes.OK
    return int(args.handler(args))
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `tests/conftest.py` with the media skip rule**

```python
"""Shared test setup.

Tests marked `media` need ffmpeg and ffprobe. They skip without them, unless
VID2SUB_REQUIRE_FFMPEG=1 (CI sets it), when they fail instead: a skipped guard is no guard.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import os
import shutil

import pytest


def pytest_collection_modifyitems(config, items):
    have = shutil.which("ffmpeg") and shutil.which("ffprobe")
    if have or os.environ.get("VID2SUB_REQUIRE_FFMPEG") == "1":
        return
    skip = pytest.mark.skip(reason="ffmpeg/ffprobe not installed")
    for item in items:
        if "media" in item.keywords:
            item.add_marker(skip)
```

```python
import subprocess
import sys

import pytest

from vid2sub.cli import main


def test_vid2sub_with_no_arguments_prints_help_and_exits_0(capsys):
    assert main([]) == 0
    assert "usage: vid2sub" in capsys.readouterr().out


def test_python_dash_m_vid2sub_prints_the_version():
    proc = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-m", "vid2sub", "--version"],
                          capture_output=True, text=True)
    assert proc.returncode == 0
    assert proc.stdout.startswith("vid2sub ")


def test_a_usage_error_exits_64_not_2():
    with pytest.raises(SystemExit) as err:
        main(["--no-such-option"])
    assert err.value.code == 64
```

`CLAUDE.md`:
```markdown
# vid2sub

A Python CLI (and, from M2, a local web app) that turns videos and YouTube URLs into subtitle
files next to the video. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-vid2sub-design.md`; current
plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/`.

## ⛔ Hard rules

- **Never read or print secrets.** Keys are used by env var name (`key_env` in `vid2sub.toml`).
  Check a variable with `python -c "import os; print(bool(os.environ.get('X')))"`, never its value.
- **Never overwrite an artifact.** The sidecar is append-only; subtitle files are rewritten only
  when the sidecar has a projection record for them (spec §14, §15.3).
- **Pure packages stay pure:** `vid2sub.subs`, `vid2sub.parse`, `vid2sub.catalog` (spec §3.2).
  `tests/test_purity.py` enforces it.
- **One client factory:** only `src/vid2sub/engines/clients.py` imports openai, httpx or chatlas.
- **No titles, paths, tokens or keys in logs or `--json` output** (spec §6.8).
- **Everything runs through uv** (`uv run …`); never `pip install`.

## Commands

- `make setup` · `make test` · `make lint` · `make live` (opt-in tests against real models).

## Check the docs, never write from memory

These change quickly. Before writing code that uses one, read its current docs; say in the
commit what you checked. If the docs don't cover it, say so instead of guessing.

| Library | Docs |
|---|---|
| chatlas | https://posit-dev.github.io/chatlas/ (or the context7 docs tool) |
| openai (transcriptions) | https://platform.openai.com/docs/api-reference/audio |
| pysubs2 | https://pysubs2.readthedocs.io/ |
| yt-dlp | https://github.com/yt-dlp/yt-dlp#usage-and-options |
| whisper.cpp server | https://github.com/ggml-org/whisper.cpp/tree/master/examples/server |
| mlx-qwen3-asr | https://github.com/moona3k/mlx-qwen3-asr |
| opencc-purepy | https://pypi.org/project/opencc-purepy/ |
| onnxruntime | https://onnxruntime.ai/docs/api/python/ |

## Style

- Module docstrings: one sentence of purpose, then why, citing spec sections and who decided.
- British spelling. Test names are sentences. Guard tests must fail without their fix.
- Commits: `type(scope): plain behavioural sentence`; the body says what was checked.
```

`tests/test_langs.py`:
```python
import pytest

from vid2sub.subs import langs


def test_the_four_languages_dan_needs_are_known_and_marked_cjk_correctly():
    assert {"en", "ja", "zh", "yue"} <= set(langs.LANGS)
    assert not langs.get("en").cjk
    assert langs.get("ja").cjk and langs.get("zh").cjk and langs.get("yue").cjk


def test_an_unknown_language_names_the_known_ones():
    with pytest.raises(langs.UnknownLanguage, match="known: en"):
        langs.get("xx")


def test_whisper_cpp_full_names_and_qwen_names_map_back_to_codes():
    assert langs.from_whisper_full("japanese") == "ja"
    assert langs.from_whisper_full("Cantonese") == "yue"
    assert langs.from_qwen("Chinese") == "zh"
    assert langs.from_qwen("Cantonese") == "yue"
    assert langs.from_qwen("Klingon") is None


def test_media_language_tags_map_to_codes():
    assert langs.from_media_tag("jpn") == "ja"
    assert langs.from_media_tag("chi") == "zh"
    assert langs.from_media_tag("ENG") == "en"
    assert langs.from_media_tag("und") is None
    assert langs.from_media_tag(None) is None
```

`tests/test_purity.py`:
```python
"""The pure packages run in Pyodide later (spec §3.2, §21), so they may import only the standard
library (minus processes and networking), pydantic and the subtitle libraries."""

import ast
import sys
from pathlib import Path

SRC = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "src" / "vid2sub"
PURE_FILES = [
    *sorted((SRC / "subs").rglob("*.py")),
    *sorted((SRC / "parse").rglob("*.py")),
    *([SRC / "catalog.py"] if (SRC / "catalog.py").exists() else []),
]
THIRD_PARTY = {"pydantic", "pysubs2", "opencc_purepy", "charset_normalizer"}
FORBIDDEN_STDLIB = {"subprocess", "socket", "http", "urllib", "asyncio", "threading",
                    "multiprocessing", "ssl", "ctypes"}
PURE_INTERNAL = ("vid2sub.subs", "vid2sub.parse", "vid2sub.catalog")


def _imports(path: Path):
    for node in ast.walk(ast.parse(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))):
        if isinstance(node, ast.Import):
            yield from (alias.name for alias in node.names)
        elif isinstance(node, ast.ImportFrom) and node.level == 0 and node.module:
            yield node.module


def test_pure_modules_import_only_the_standard_library_pydantic_and_subtitle_libraries():
    assert PURE_FILES, "no pure modules found"
    bad = []
    for path in PURE_FILES:
        for name in _imports(path):
            root = name.split(".")[0]
            if name == "__future__":
                continue
            if root == "vid2sub":
                if not name.startswith(PURE_INTERNAL):
                    bad.append(f"{path.name}: {name}")
            elif root in FORBIDDEN_STDLIB or (
                root not in sys.stdlib_module_names and root not in THIRD_PARTY
            ):
                bad.append(f"{path.name}: {name}")
    assert not bad, "impure imports in pure modules:\n" + "\n".join(bad)
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`src/vid2sub/subs/__init__.py`:
```python
"""The pure subtitle model (spec §3.2): no processes, no network, runs in Pyodide later."""
```

`src/vid2sub/subs/langs.py`:
```python
"""The languages vid2sub knows, and the name every tool uses for each (spec §2.2, §9.2, §11).

One table, so a language is added in one place. Codes are vid2sub's own: ISO 639-1 where one
exists, else ISO 639-3 (`yue`, Cantonese, which Whisper also calls `yue`). whisper.cpp's
verbose_json names languages in full ("cantonese"); Qwen3-ASR wants capitalised English names
("Cantonese"); media files tag tracks with ISO 639-2 (`jpn`, `chi`).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Lang:
    code: str
    name: str
    whisper_full: str
    qwen: str
    cjk: bool
    media_tags: tuple[str, ...]


LANGS: dict[str, Lang] = {
    lang.code: lang
    for lang in (
        Lang("en", "English", "english", "English", False, ("eng", "en")),
        Lang("ja", "Japanese", "japanese", "Japanese", True, ("jpn", "ja")),
        Lang("zh", "Mandarin", "chinese", "Chinese", True, ("chi", "zho", "cmn", "zh")),
        Lang("yue", "Cantonese", "cantonese", "Cantonese", True, ("yue",)),
        Lang("ko", "Korean", "korean", "Korean", True, ("kor", "ko")),
    )
}


class UnknownLanguage(ValueError):
    pass


def get(code: str) -> Lang:
    try:
        return LANGS[code]
    except KeyError:
        raise UnknownLanguage(
            f"unknown language {code!r}; known: {', '.join(LANGS)}") from None


def from_whisper_full(name: str) -> str | None:
    name = name.strip().lower()
    return next((lang.code for lang in LANGS.values() if lang.whisper_full == name), None)


def from_qwen(name: str) -> str | None:
    name = name.strip().lower()
    return next((lang.code for lang in LANGS.values() if lang.qwen.lower() == name), None)


def from_media_tag(tag: str | None) -> str | None:
    if not tag:
        return None
    tag = tag.strip().lower()
    return next((lang.code for lang in LANGS.values() if tag in lang.media_tags), None)
```

```python
    assert t.width("日本") == 4
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`src/vid2sub/subs/text.py`:
```python
"""Text rules for subtitle lines (spec §12.2, §12.3, §15.2).

Width is in East Asian Width units: a wide or full-width character counts 2, anything else 1, so
one limit table covers English (42) and Japanese (26, i.e. 13 full-width characters), as
Netflix's timed-text guides count them. Lines break at spaces, or between two characters where
either is CJK; never inside a run of Latin letters or digits, and never against kinsoku: no line
starts with closing punctuation or small kana, and none ends with an opening bracket.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
import unicodedata

NO_LINE_START = frozenset(
    "、。，．・：；？！ー…‥）」』】〕〉》〙〗〟’”｠»ゝゞヽヾぁぃぅぇぉっゃゅょゎゕゖ"
    "ァィゥェォッャュョヮヵヶ々〻,.!?;:)]}%％"
)
NO_LINE_END = frozenset("（「『【〔〈《〘〖〝‘“｟«([{")
SENTENCE_END = frozenset(".!?。！？…")
CLAUSE_END = frozenset(",;:、，；：")
_SPACES = re.compile(r"[ \t]+")


def char_width(ch: str) -> int:
    if unicodedata.combining(ch):
        return 0
    return 2 if unicodedata.east_asian_width(ch) in ("W", "F") else 1


def width(text: str) -> int:
    return sum(char_width(ch) for ch in text)


def is_cjk(ch: str) -> bool:
    return unicodedata.east_asian_width(ch) in ("W", "F")


def canonical(text: str) -> str:
    """`\\n` line breaks, single spaces, no blank or padded lines (spec §15.2)."""
    text = text.replace("\r\n", "\n").replace("\r", "\n")
    lines = (_SPACES.sub(" ", line).strip() for line in text.split("\n"))
    return "\n".join(line for line in lines if line)


def unwrap(text: str) -> str:
    """Join lines into one: a space between Latin text, nothing between CJK."""
    parts = canonical(text).split("\n")
    out = parts[0]
    for part in parts[1:]:
        joiner = "" if out and is_cjk(out[-1]) and is_cjk(part[0]) else " "
        out += joiner + part
    return out


def _breaks(text: str) -> list[tuple[int, int]]:
    """Where `text` may break: (end of line 1, start of line 2)."""
    points: list[tuple[int, int]] = []
    for i in range(1, len(text)):
        prev, cur = text[i - 1], text[i]
        if cur == " ":
            continue
        if prev == " ":
            j = i - 1
            while j > 0 and text[j - 1] == " ":
                j -= 1
            if j > 0:
                points.append((j, i))
            continue
        if not (is_cjk(prev) or is_cjk(cur)):
            continue
        if cur in NO_LINE_START or prev in NO_LINE_END:
            continue
        points.append((i, i))
    return points


def wrap(text: str, max_width: int, max_lines: int = 2) -> list[str] | None:
    """At most two lines no wider than `max_width`, as balanced as possible; None if impossible.

    Ties prefer a shorter first line, and a break after punctuation wins over a slightly more
    even one."""
    text = unwrap(text)
    if not text:
        return []
    if width(text) <= max_width:
        return [text]
    if max_lines < 2:
        return None
    best: tuple[float, list[str]] | None = None
    for end1, start2 in _breaks(text):
        first, second = text[:end1].rstrip(), text[start2:].lstrip()
        if not first or not second:
            continue
        w1, w2 = width(first), width(second)
        if w1 > max_width or w2 > max_width:
            continue
        score = abs(w1 - w2) + (0.5 if w1 > w2 else 0.0)
        if first[-1] in SENTENCE_END or first[-1] in CLAUSE_END:
            score -= 4
        if best is None or score < best[0]:
            best = (score, [first, second])
    return best[1] if best else None


def strip_line_ends(lines: list[str], chars: str = "，。") -> list[str]:
    """Chinese subtitle lines don't end with a comma or full stop (spec §12.3)."""
    return [line.rstrip(chars) or line for line in lines]
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`src/vid2sub/subs/model.py`:
```python
"""The subtitle data model: words, segments, transcripts and cues (spec §9.2, §12.1, §14.3).

Times are integer milliseconds everywhere. Words follow Whisper's convention, whatever engine
produced them: a leading space marks a word boundary, so joining a segment's words gives its text,
and CJK words carry no spaces. A word whose time the engine didn't give is `timed=False` and sits
at the previous word's end (recordings' rule). Cue ids are random, unique per track and never
reused; SRT numbers are only for display.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import secrets
from collections.abc import Sequence
from typing import Any

from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field


class Word(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(frozen=True, extra="forbid")
    text: str
    start_ms: int = Field(ge=0)
    end_ms: int = Field(ge=0)
    prob: float | None = None
    timed: bool = True


class Segment(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(frozen=True, extra="forbid")
    text: str
    start_ms: int = Field(ge=0)
    end_ms: int = Field(ge=0)
    words: tuple[Word, ...] = ()
    avg_logprob: float | None = None
    no_speech_prob: float | None = None
    compression_ratio: float | None = None

    @property
    def aligned(self) -> bool:
        return bool(self.words)


class Transcript(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(frozen=True, extra="forbid")
    language: str
    segments: tuple[Segment, ...]
    language_probs: dict[str, float] = Field(default_factory=dict)

    def words(self) -> list[Word]:
        return [word for seg in self.segments for word in seg.words]


class Cue(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(frozen=True, extra="forbid", populate_by_name=True)
    id: str
    start_ms: int = Field(ge=0)
    end_ms: int = Field(ge=0)
    text: str
    w: tuple[int, int] | None = None  # word index range [i, j) in the transcript
    src: tuple[str, ...] | None = None  # translation: the source cue ids it covers
    src_sha: str | None = None
    from_: tuple[str, ...] | None = Field(default=None, alias="from")
    detached: bool = False
    flags: tuple[str, ...] = ()

    def dump(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        return self.model_dump(mode="json", by_alias=True, exclude_defaults=True)


def shift_segment(seg: Segment, offset_ms: int) -> Segment:
    return seg.model_copy(update={
        "start_ms": seg.start_ms + offset_ms,
        "end_ms": seg.end_ms + offset_ms,
        "words": tuple(w.model_copy(update={"start_ms": w.start_ms + offset_ms,
                                            "end_ms": w.end_ms + offset_ms})
                       for w in seg.words),
    })


def new_cue_id(taken: set[str]) -> str:
    while True:
        cid = "c" + secrets.token_hex(4)
        if cid not in taken:
            taken.add(cid)
            return cid


class TrackError(ValueError):
    pass


def check_track(cues: Sequence[Cue]) -> None:
    """The invariants every stored track keeps (spec §12.1)."""
    seen: set[str] = set()
    prev: Cue | None = None
    for c in cues:
        if c.id in seen:
            raise TrackError(f"duplicate id {c.id}")
        seen.add(c.id)
        if c.start_ms >= c.end_ms:
            raise TrackError(f"cue {c.id} starts at or after its end")
        if not c.text.strip():
            raise TrackError(f"cue {c.id} is empty")
        if any(not line.strip() for line in c.text.split("\n")):
            raise TrackError(f"cue {c.id} has a blank line")
        if prev is not None:
            if c.start_ms < prev.start_ms:
                raise TrackError(f"cue {c.id} is out of order")
            if c.start_ms < prev.end_ms:
                raise TrackError(f"cue {c.id} overlaps {prev.id}")
        prev = c
```

```python
from vid2sub.subs.model import Cue
from vid2sub.subs.rules import RULES, cps, rules_for, width_chars


def test_rules_follow_the_netflix_line_widths_per_language():
    assert rules_for("en").max_width == 42
    assert rules_for("ja").max_width == 26
    assert rules_for("zh").max_width == 32 and rules_for("yue").max_width == 32
    assert rules_for("xx") == RULES["en"]
    assert width_chars(rules_for("ja"), cjk=True) == 13


def test_the_rules_hash_changes_when_a_rule_changes():
    en = rules_for("en")
    assert en.sha() == rules_for("en").sha()
    assert en.sha() != rules_for("ja").sha()


def test_reading_speed_ignores_line_breaks():
    assert cps("ab\ncd", 1000) == 4.0


def test_known_hallucinations_are_recognised_across_lines_and_case():
    assert looks_hallucinated("ご視聴\nありがとうございました", "ja")
    assert looks_hallucinated("Thank you\nfor watching!", "en")
    assert looks_hallucinated("Thanks for watching", "ja")  # English phrases count everywhere
    assert not looks_hallucinated("We choose to go to the moon", "en")


def test_lint_flags_width_duration_and_reading_speed():
    rules = rules_for("en")
    cues = [
        Cue(id="ok", start_ms=0, end_ms=2000, text="Fine"),
        Cue(id="wide", start_ms=3000, end_ms=6000, text="x" * 50),
        Cue(id="short", start_ms=7000, end_ms=7300, text="Hi"),
        Cue(id="fast", start_ms=8000, end_ms=9000, text="This is far too much text to read"),
    ]
    flags = lint(cues, rules)
    assert "ok" not in flags
    assert "width" in flags["wide"]
    assert "duration" in flags["short"]
    assert "cps" in flags["fast"]
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run them to see them fail**

Run: `uv run pytest tests/test_rules_lint.py -v`
Expected: FAIL (`ModuleNotFoundError`).

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`src/vid2sub/subs/rules.py`:
```python
"""Cue rules per language (spec §12.2), defaulting to Netflix's timed-text style guides.

Width is in East Asian Width units (Task 3): English 42; Japanese 26 (13 full-width); Chinese and
Cantonese 32 (16). Reading speed (characters per second) only extends cues into gaps and lints;
it never splits or merges (spec §12.2), because verbatim Japanese can't meet 4 cps. The rules'
hash goes into cue versions, so changing a rule mints a new version.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import json
from dataclasses import asdict, dataclass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CueRules:
    max_width: int
    cps: float
    max_lines: int = 2
    min_ms: int = 833
    max_ms: int = 7000
    min_gap_ms: int = 84  # two frames at 23.976 fps
    close_gap_ms: int = 500
    pause_ms: int = 700  # a longer silence ends a phrase

    def sha(self) -> str:
        blob = json.dumps(asdict(self), sort_keys=True).encode()
        return hashlib.sha256(blob).hexdigest()[:8]


RULES: dict[str, CueRules] = {
    "en": CueRules(max_width=42, cps=20.0),
    "ja": CueRules(max_width=26, cps=4.0),
    "zh": CueRules(max_width=32, cps=9.0),
    "yue": CueRules(max_width=32, cps=9.0),
    "ko": CueRules(max_width=32, cps=12.0),
}


def rules_for(lang: str) -> CueRules:
    return RULES.get(lang, RULES["en"])


def reading_chars(text: str) -> int:
    return len(text.replace("\n", ""))


def cps(text: str, duration_ms: int) -> float:
    return reading_chars(text) * 1000 / max(duration_ms, 1)


def width_chars(rules: CueRules, cjk: bool) -> int:
    """Characters per line: half the width for full-width text."""
    return rules.max_width // 2 if cjk else rules.max_width
```

`src/vid2sub/subs/lint.py`:
```python
"""Lint flags and known hallucinations (spec §12.4, §12.5).

Lint flags are recomputed whenever they're shown, never stored as truth. Hallucination phrases are
the lines Whisper-family models invent over silence and music, per language; English ones are
checked in every language because they turn up everywhere. A flagged cue is shown, never deleted.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Sequence

from vid2sub.subs.model import Cue
from vid2sub.subs.rules import CueRules, cps
from vid2sub.subs.text import unwrap, width

HALLUCINATION_PHRASES: dict[str, tuple[str, ...]] = {
    "en": ("thank you for watching", "thanks for watching", "please subscribe",
           "subtitles by the amara.org community"),
    "ja": ("ご視聴ありがとうございました", "チャンネル登録をお願いします"),
    "zh": ("字幕由amara.org社区提供", "请不吝点赞", "谢谢观看", "感谢观看"),
    "yue": ("字幕由amara.org社區提供", "多謝收睇", "多謝收看"),
    "ko": ("시청해 주셔서 감사합니다",),
}


def looks_hallucinated(text: str, lang: str) -> bool:
    norm = unwrap(text).lower()
    phrases = HALLUCINATION_PHRASES.get(lang, ()) + HALLUCINATION_PHRASES["en"]
    return any(phrase in norm for phrase in phrases)


def lint(cues: Sequence[Cue], rules: CueRules) -> dict[str, tuple[str, ...]]:
    out: dict[str, tuple[str, ...]] = {}
    for c in cues:
        flags: list[str] = []
        lines = c.text.split("\n")
        if len(lines) > rules.max_lines or any(width(line) > rules.max_width for line in lines):
            flags.append("width")
        duration = c.end_ms - c.start_ms
        if duration < rules.min_ms or duration > rules.max_ms:
            flags.append("duration")
        if cps(c.text, duration) > rules.cps:
            flags.append("cps")
        if flags:
            out[c.id] = tuple(flags)
    return out
```

### Task 6: Building cues from words

**Files:**
- Create: `src/vid2sub/subs/build.py`

**Interfaces:**

  SENTENCE_END, CLAUSE_END` (Task 3); `langs` (Task 2).
- Produces: `build_cues(transcript: Transcript, rules: CueRules, *,
  speech: Sequence[tuple[int, int]] | None = None, taken: set[str] | None = None) -> list[Cue]`.
  Cues come back satisfying `check_track`; aligned cues carry `w=(i, j)`, the word index range in
  `transcript.words()`; unaligned ones carry flag `"est"`; suspects carry `"halluc"`.

`tests/test_build.py`:
```python
from hypothesis import given, settings
from hypothesis import strategies as st

from vid2sub.subs.build import build_cues
from vid2sub.subs.model import Segment, Transcript, Word, check_track
from vid2sub.subs.rules import rules_for
from vid2sub.subs.text import width


def words(texts, start=0, step=400, dur=350):
    return tuple(Word(text=t, start_ms=start + i * step, end_ms=start + i * step + dur)
                 for i, t in enumerate(texts))


def seg(ws, **kw):
    return Segment(text="".join(w.text for w in ws), start_ms=ws[0].start_ms,
                   end_ms=ws[-1].end_ms, words=ws, **kw)


def transcript(lang, *segments):
    return Transcript(language=lang, segments=segments)


def test_a_short_english_sentence_becomes_one_cue_covering_every_word():
    ws = words([" We", " choose", " to", " go", " to", " the", " moon."])
    cues = build_cues(transcript("en", seg(ws)), rules_for("en"))
    assert [c.text for c in cues] == ["We choose to go to the moon."]
    assert cues[0].w == (0, 7)
    assert cues[0].start_ms == 0


def test_a_long_pause_starts_a_new_cue():
    first = words([" Hello", " there."])
    second = words([" How", " are", " you?"], start=3000)
    cues = build_cues(transcript("en", seg(first + second)), rules_for("en"))
    assert [c.text for c in cues] == ["Hello there.", "How are you?"]


def test_a_long_run_of_speech_is_split_into_cues_that_fit():
    ws = words([" word"] * 80, step=450)
    cues = build_cues(transcript("en", seg(ws)), rules_for("en"))
    check_track(cues)
    assert len(cues) > 3
    for c in cues:
        assert all(width(line) <= 42 for line in c.text.split("\n"))
        assert c.end_ms - c.start_ms <= 7000 + 500


def test_japanese_words_join_without_spaces_and_wrap_within_26():
    text = "今日はいい天気ですね明日も晴れるといいなと思っていますよ"
    ws = words(list(text), step=200, dur=180)
    cues = build_cues(transcript("ja", seg(ws)), rules_for("ja"))
    check_track(cues)
    assert " " not in "".join(c.text for c in cues)
    assert all(width(line) <= 26 for c in cues for line in c.text.split("\n"))


def test_chinese_lines_drop_a_trailing_comma_or_full_stop():
    ws = words(["你", "好", "，", "世", "界", "。"], step=300)
    cues = build_cues(transcript("zh", seg(ws)), rules_for("zh"))
    assert not any(line.endswith(("，", "。")) for c in cues for line in c.text.split("\n"))


def test_an_unaligned_segment_is_spread_over_its_time_and_flagged_estimated():
    s = Segment(text="This segment lost its word timings because of a temperature fallback "
                     "and has to be estimated from the segment instead of the words",
                start_ms=10_000, end_ms=20_000)
    cues = build_cues(transcript("en", s), rules_for("en"))
    check_track(cues)
    assert len(cues) >= 2
    assert all("est" in c.flags for c in cues)
    assert cues[0].start_ms == 10_000 and cues[-1].end_ms == 20_000


def test_a_lone_short_word_is_stretched_to_the_minimum_duration():
    cues = build_cues(transcript("en", seg(words([" Yes."], dur=200))), rules_for("en"))
    assert cues[0].end_ms - cues[0].start_ms >= 833


def test_a_gap_under_half_a_second_is_closed_to_two_frames():
    first = words([" One", " two", " three."])
    second = words([" Four."], start=first[-1].end_ms + 300)
    cues = build_cues(transcript("en", seg(first), seg(second)), rules_for("en"))
    assert cues[1].start_ms - cues[0].end_ms == 84


def test_a_known_hallucination_is_flagged_not_deleted():
    ws = words(["ご", "視", "聴", "あ", "り", "が", "と", "う", "ご", "ざ", "い", "ま", "し", "た"],
               step=200)
    cues = build_cues(transcript("ja", seg(ws)), rules_for("ja"))
    assert cues and all("halluc" in c.flags for c in cues)


def test_words_spoken_where_the_vad_heard_no_speech_are_flagged():
    ws = words([" Mystery", " words", " here."], start=50_000)
    cues = build_cues(transcript("en", seg(ws)), rules_for("en"), speech=[(0, 10_000)])
    assert "halluc" in cues[0].flags


@settings(max_examples=60, deadline=None)
@given(st.lists(st.tuples(st.integers(0, 2500), st.integers(30, 1500),
                          st.sampled_from([" word", " the.", " end,", "字", "。"])),
                min_size=1, max_size=120))
def test_any_word_sequence_gives_a_valid_track_that_covers_every_word_once(spec):
    t, ws = 0, []
    for gap, dur, text in spec:
        t += gap
        ws.append(Word(text=text, start_ms=t, end_ms=t + dur))
        t += dur
    cues = build_cues(transcript("en", seg(tuple(ws))), rules_for("en"))
    check_track(cues)
    covered = sorted(i for c in cues if c.w for i in range(*c.w))
    assert covered == list(range(len(ws)))
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`src/vid2sub/subs/build.py`:
```python
"""Cues from words (spec §12).

Words are grouped into phrases at sentence ends and pauses, then packed greedily into cues that
fit two lines and seven seconds, preferring to cut after clause punctuation. Timing is then fixed
up: overlaps removed, gaps under half a second closed to two frames, short cues stretched to the
minimum and fast ones into the following gap. A segment whose words lost their times (Whisper's
temperature fallback, an audio-router lesson) is spread over its own span in proportion to
characters and flagged `est`. Suspected hallucinations are flagged `halluc`, never removed.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Sequence
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

from vid2sub.subs import langs
from vid2sub.subs.lint import looks_hallucinated
from vid2sub.subs.model import Cue, Segment, Transcript, Word, new_cue_id
from vid2sub.subs.rules import CueRules, cps, reading_chars
from vid2sub.subs.text import CLAUSE_END, SENTENCE_END, canonical, strip_line_ends, wrap

HALLUC_COMPRESSION = 2.4  # Whisper's own thresholds
HALLUC_NO_SPEECH = 0.6
HALLUC_LOGPROB = -1.0
_TRAILING_QUOTES = "\"'”’」』)）"


@dataclass
class _Draft:
    start: int
    end: int
    text: str
    w: tuple[int, int] | None
    flags: set[str] = field(default_factory=set)


def _cjk(lang: str) -> bool:
    return lang in langs.LANGS and langs.get(lang).cjk


def _join(words: Sequence[Word]) -> str:
    return "".join(w.text for w in words).strip()


def _lines(text: str, rules: CueRules, lang: str) -> str:
    lines = wrap(text, rules.max_width, rules.max_lines) or [text]
    if lang in ("zh", "yue"):
        lines = strip_line_ends(lines)
    return "\n".join(lines)


def _fits(words: Sequence[Word], rules: CueRules) -> bool:
    return (words[-1].end_ms - words[0].start_ms <= rules.max_ms
            and wrap(_join(words), rules.max_width, rules.max_lines) is not None)


def _ends(word: Word, chars: frozenset[str]) -> bool:
    text = word.text.rstrip().rstrip(_TRAILING_QUOTES)
    return bool(text) and text[-1] in chars


Item = tuple[int, Word]


def _phrases(items: list[Item], rules: CueRules) -> list[list[Item]]:
    phrases: list[list[Item]] = []
    cur: list[Item] = []
    for item in items:
        if cur:
            prev = cur[-1][1]
            if item[1].start_ms - prev.end_ms > rules.pause_ms or _ends(prev, SENTENCE_END):
                phrases.append(cur)
                cur = []
        cur.append(item)
    if cur:
        phrases.append(cur)
    return phrases


def _pack(phrase: list[Item], rules: CueRules) -> list[list[Item]]:
    groups: list[list[Item]] = []
    cur: list[Item] = []
    for item in phrase:
        candidate = cur + [item]
        if not cur or _fits([w for _, w in candidate], rules):
            cur = candidate
            continue
        lowest = max(len(cur) // 2, 1)
        cut = next((i for i in range(len(cur) - 1, lowest - 1, -1)
                    if _ends(cur[i - 1][1], CLAUSE_END)), None)
        if cut is not None:
            groups.append(cur[:cut])
            cur = cur[cut:] + [item]
            if not _fits([w for _, w in cur], rules):
                groups.append(cur[:-1])
                cur = [item]
        else:
            groups.append(cur)
            cur = [item]
    if cur:
        groups.append(cur)
    return groups


def _unaligned(seg: Segment, rules: CueRules, lang: str) -> list[_Draft]:
    text = canonical(seg.text).replace("\n", " ").strip()
    if not text:
        return []
    cjk = _cjk(lang)
    tokens = list(text) if cjk else text.split(" ")
    joiner = "" if cjk else " "
    pieces: list[str] = []
    cur: list[str] = []
    for tok in tokens:
        candidate = cur + [tok]
        if cur and wrap(joiner.join(candidate), rules.max_width, rules.max_lines) is None:
            pieces.append(joiner.join(cur))
            cur = [tok]
        else:
            cur = candidate
    if cur:
        pieces.append(joiner.join(cur))
    pieces = [p.strip() for p in pieces if p.strip()]
    total = sum(reading_chars(p) for p in pieces) or 1
    span = max(seg.end_ms - seg.start_ms, 1)
    drafts: list[_Draft] = []
    t = seg.start_ms
    for i, piece in enumerate(pieces):
        last = i == len(pieces) - 1
        end = seg.end_ms if last else t + round(span * reading_chars(piece) / total)
        drafts.append(_Draft(t, max(end, t + 1), _lines(piece, rules, lang), None, {"est"}))
        t = end
    return drafts


def _in_speech(t: int, speech: Sequence[tuple[int, int]]) -> bool:
    return any(s <= t < e for s, e in speech)


def _suspect(draft: _Draft, seg: Segment, words: Sequence[Word], lang: str,
             speech: Sequence[tuple[int, int]] | None) -> bool:
    if looks_hallucinated(draft.text, lang):
        return True
    if seg.compression_ratio is not None and seg.compression_ratio > HALLUC_COMPRESSION:
        return True
    if (seg.no_speech_prob is not None and seg.avg_logprob is not None
            and seg.no_speech_prob > HALLUC_NO_SPEECH and seg.avg_logprob < HALLUC_LOGPROB):
        return True
    if speech is not None and words:
        outside = sum(1 for w in words if not _in_speech((w.start_ms + w.end_ms) // 2, speech))
        return outside * 2 > len(words)
    return False


def _fix_timing(drafts: list[_Draft], rules: CueRules) -> None:
    drafts.sort(key=lambda d: (d.start, d.end))
    for i, d in enumerate(drafts):
        nxt = drafts[i + 1].start if i + 1 < len(drafts) else None
        limit = nxt - rules.min_gap_ms if nxt is not None else None
        if nxt is not None and limit is not None and (
                d.end > limit or nxt - d.end < rules.close_gap_ms):
            d.end = limit
        if d.end - d.start < rules.min_ms:
            want = d.start + rules.min_ms
            d.end = want if limit is None else max(d.end, min(want, limit))
        if cps(d.text, d.end - d.start) > rules.cps:
            want = d.start + min(rules.max_ms, round(reading_chars(d.text) * 1000 / rules.cps))
            d.end = max(d.end, want if limit is None else min(want, limit))
        if d.end <= d.start:
            d.end = d.start + 1


def _untangle(drafts: list[_Draft]) -> None:
    """Last resort for cues that start together: never let two overlap."""
    for a, b in zip(drafts, drafts[1:]):
        if b.start < a.end:
            b.start = a.end
        if b.end <= b.start:
            b.end = b.start + 1


def build_cues(transcript: Transcript, rules: CueRules, *,
               speech: Sequence[tuple[int, int]] | None = None,
               taken: set[str] | None = None) -> list[Cue]:
    lang = transcript.language
    drafts: list[_Draft] = []
    index = 0
    for seg in transcript.segments:
        if not seg.words:
            for d in _unaligned(seg, rules, lang):
                if looks_hallucinated(d.text, lang):
                    d.flags.add("halluc")
                drafts.append(d)
            continue
        items = [(index + k, w) for k, w in enumerate(seg.words)]
        index += len(seg.words)
        for phrase in _phrases(items, rules):
            for group in _pack(phrase, rules):
                ws = [w for _, w in group]
                text = _lines(_join(ws), rules, lang)
                d = _Draft(ws[0].start_ms, max(ws[-1].end_ms, ws[0].start_ms + 1), text,
                           (group[0][0], group[-1][0] + 1))
                if _suspect(d, seg, ws, lang, speech):
                    d.flags.add("halluc")
                drafts.append(d)
    drafts = [d for d in drafts if d.text.strip()]
    _fix_timing(drafts, rules)
    _untangle(drafts)
    taken = set() if taken is None else taken
    return [Cue(id=new_cue_id(taken), start_ms=d.start, end_ms=d.end, text=d.text, w=d.w,
                flags=tuple(sorted(d.flags))) for d in drafts]
```

```python
import pytest

from vid2sub.subs import formats as f
from vid2sub.subs.model import Cue

CUES = [Cue(id="a", start_ms=1000, end_ms=2500, text="Hello\nworld"),
        Cue(id="b", start_ms=3000, end_ms=4000, text="<i>Bye</i>")]


def test_srt_bytes_use_lf_and_utf8_and_are_stable():
    data = f.to_srt(CUES)
    text = data.decode("utf-8")
    assert "\r" not in text
    assert text.startswith("1\n00:00:01,000 --> 00:00:02,500\nHello\nworld\n")
    assert "<i>Bye</i>" in text
    assert f.sha256_hex(data) == f.sha256_hex(f.to_srt(CUES))


def test_a_bom_is_added_only_when_asked():
    assert f.to_srt(CUES, bom=True).startswith(b"\xef\xbb\xbf")
    assert not f.to_srt(CUES).startswith(b"\xef\xbb\xbf")


def test_srt_round_trips_text_italics_and_times():
    parsed = f.parse_subtitles(f.to_srt(CUES), "srt")
    assert [(p.start_ms, p.end_ms, p.text) for p in parsed] == [
        (1000, 2500, "Hello\nworld"), (3000, 4000, "<i>Bye</i>")]


def test_ass_round_trips_at_centisecond_precision():
    cues = [Cue(id="a", start_ms=1234, end_ms=2567, text="One\ntwo")]
    parsed = f.parse_subtitles(f.to_ass(cues), "ass")
    assert parsed[0].text == "One\ntwo"
    assert f.same_cues(parsed, cues, precision_ms=f.PRECISION_MS["ass"])
    assert not f.same_cues(parsed, cues, precision_ms=1)


def test_braces_in_text_cant_become_ass_override_tags():
    data = f.to_ass([Cue(id="a", start_ms=0, end_ms=1000, text="{not a tag}")]).decode()
    assert "{not a tag}" not in data
    assert "｛not a tag｝" in data


def test_rewriting_an_ass_file_keeps_its_styles_info_and_other_events():
    first = f.to_ass(CUES).decode()
    edited = first.replace("[Events]", "[Events]").replace(
        "Title: ", "Title: My show") + \
        "Dialogue: 0,0:00:05.00,0:00:06.00,Sign,,0,0,0,,A sign\n"
    edited = edited.replace(
        "[Events]",
        "[V4+ Styles]\nFormat: Name, Fontname, Fontsize, PrimaryColour, SecondaryColour, "
        "OutlineColour, BackColour, Bold, Italic, Underline, StrikeOut, ScaleX, ScaleY, Spacing, "
        "Angle, BorderStyle, Outline, Shadow, Alignment, MarginL, MarginR, MarginV, Encoding\n"
        "Style: Sign,Arial,40,&H00FFFFFF,&H000000FF,&H00000000,&H00000000,0,0,0,0,100,100,0,0,"
        "1,2,0,8,10,10,10,1\n\n[Events]", 1)
    out = f.to_ass([Cue(id="a", start_ms=1000, end_ms=2000, text="New")],
                   existing=edited.encode()).decode()
    assert "Sign" in out and "A sign" in out
    assert "New" in out and "Hello" not in out


def test_foreign_markup_is_stripped_on_import():
    srt = "1\n00:00:01,000 --> 00:00:02,000\n<font color=\"red\">Red</font> {\\an8}top\n\n"
    assert f.parse_subtitles(srt.encode(), "srt")[0].text == "Red top"


@pytest.mark.parametrize("encoding, line", [
    ("gbk", "今天天气很好，我们一起去公园散步吧。"),
    ("big5", "今天天氣很好，我們一起去公園散步吧。"),
    ("shift_jis", "今日はとても良い天気なので、公園を散歩しましょう。"),
])
def test_legacy_east_asian_encodings_are_detected(encoding, line):
    body = "".join(f"{i}\n00:00:0{i},000 --> 00:00:0{i},900\n{line}\n\n" for i in range(1, 6))
    parsed = f.parse_subtitles(body.encode(encoding), "srt")
    assert parsed[0].text == line


def test_an_unreadable_file_raises_a_subtitle_error():
    with pytest.raises(f.SubtitleError):
        f.parse_subtitles(b"\x00\x01\x02 not subtitles", "ass")
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`src/vid2sub/subs/formats.py`:
```python
"""SRT and ASS: bytes out, cues in, and the comparisons that decide external edits (spec §15).

vid2sub serialises with pysubs2 but writes the bytes itself: LF line endings and UTF-8 (BOM
optional), so a projection's hash is the same on every OS (pysubs2's own save() writes CRLF on
Windows). Canonical text keeps `\\n` breaks and `<i>` as the only markup; braces become full-width
so text can never turn into ASS override tags. An existing ASS file keeps its Script Info, Styles
and every event outside vid2sub's `Default` style (plan decision 6). Imports read UTF-8 with or
without a BOM, then fall back to charset-normalizer, because GBK, Big5 and Shift-JIS files are
common. ASS stores centiseconds, so comparisons allow the format's precision (spec §15.3).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import re
from collections.abc import Sequence
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Protocol

import pysubs2
from charset_normalizer import from_bytes

from vid2sub.subs.model import Cue
from vid2sub.subs.text import canonical

PRECISION_MS = {"srt": 1, "ass": 10}
_ASS_TAG = re.compile(r"\{[^}]*\}")
_HTML_TAG = re.compile(r"</?(?!i>)[a-zA-Z][^>]*>")


class SubtitleError(ValueError):
    pass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ParsedCue:
    start_ms: int
    end_ms: int
    text: str


class _Timed(Protocol):
    start_ms: int
    end_ms: int
    text: str


def sha256_hex(data: bytes) -> str:
    return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()


def canonical_to_ass(text: str) -> str:
    text = text.replace("{", "｛").replace("}", "｝")
    text = text.replace("<i>", r"{\i1}").replace("</i>", r"{\i0}")
    return text.replace("\n", r"\N")


def ass_to_canonical(text: str) -> str:
    text = text.replace(r"\N", "\n").replace(r"\n", "\n").replace(r"\h", " ")
    text = text.replace(r"{\i1}", "<i>").replace(r"{\i0}", "</i>")
    text = _ASS_TAG.sub("", text)
    text = _HTML_TAG.sub("", text)
    return canonical(text)


def decode_text(data: bytes) -> str:
    try:
        return data.decode("utf-8-sig")
    except UnicodeDecodeError:
        pass
    best = from_bytes(data).best()
    if best is None:
        raise SubtitleError("couldn't work out this file's text encoding")
    return str(best)


def _ssafile(cues: Sequence[Cue]) -> pysubs2.SSAFile:
    subs = pysubs2.SSAFile()
    for c in cues:
        subs.append(pysubs2.SSAEvent(start=c.start_ms, end=c.end_ms, text=canonical_to_ass(c.text)))
    return subs


def _encode(text: str, bom: bool) -> bytes:
    text = text.replace("\r\n", "\n").replace("\r", "\n")
    if not text.endswith("\n"):
        text += "\n"
    return (b"\xef\xbb\xbf" if bom else b"") + text.encode("utf-8")


def to_srt(cues: Sequence[Cue], *, bom: bool = False) -> bytes:
    return _encode(_ssafile(cues).to_string("srt"), bom)


def _default_style() -> pysubs2.SSAStyle:
    return pysubs2.SSAStyle(
        fontname="Noto Sans CJK SC", fontsize=52,
        primarycolor=pysubs2.Color(255, 255, 255, 0), outlinecolor=pysubs2.Color(0, 0, 0, 0),
        backcolor=pysubs2.Color(0, 0, 0, 128), outline=3, shadow=0,
        alignment=pysubs2.Alignment.BOTTOM_CENTER, marginv=40)


def to_ass(cues: Sequence[Cue], *, existing: bytes | None = None, bom: bool = False) -> bytes:
    subs = _ssafile(cues)
    subs.info.update({"PlayResX": "1920", "PlayResY": "1080", "WrapStyle": "2",
                      "ScaledBorderAndShadow": "yes"})
    subs.styles["Default"] = _default_style()
    if existing is not None:
        old = pysubs2.SSAFile.from_string(decode_text(existing), format_="ass")
        subs.info = dict(old.info)
        subs.styles = dict(old.styles)
        subs.styles.setdefault("Default", _default_style())
        kept = [e for e in old.events if e.style != "Default"]
        subs.events = sorted(list(subs.events) + kept, key=lambda e: (e.start, e.end))
    return _encode(subs.to_string("ass"), bom)


def parse_subtitles(data: bytes, fmt: str | None = None) -> list[ParsedCue]:
    text = decode_text(data)
    try:
        subs = pysubs2.SSAFile.from_string(text, format_=fmt)
    except Exception as err:  # noqa: BLE001 - pysubs2 raises several unrelated types
        raise SubtitleError(f"not a subtitle file vid2sub can read ({type(err).__name__})") from None
    out = []
    for ev in subs.events:
        if ev.is_comment:
            continue
        body = ass_to_canonical(ev.text)
        if body and ev.end > ev.start:
            out.append(ParsedCue(int(ev.start), int(ev.end), body))
    if not out and text.strip():
        raise SubtitleError("no subtitles found in this file")
    out.sort(key=lambda c: (c.start_ms, c.end_ms))
    return out


def same_cues(a: Sequence[_Timed], b: Sequence[_Timed], *, precision_ms: int) -> bool:
    if len(a) != len(b):
        return False
    return all(abs(x.start_ms - y.start_ms) < precision_ms
               and abs(x.end_ms - y.end_ms) < precision_ms
               and x.text == y.text for x, y in zip(a, b))
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`src/vid2sub/subs/naming.py`:
```python
"""Tracks, their file codes, and the names of the files next to a video (spec §15.1, §14.1).

A track is keyed by BCP-47 (`yue-Hant`); its file code is what Plex and Jellyfin recognise.
Jellyfin doesn't know `yue` and Plex documents ISO 639-1/2, so Cantonese files are `.zh-HK`
(council, 2026-10-14); `yue` stays in the sidecar. Cantonese is Traditional only in v1 (plan
decision 7).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import PurePath

GLOSSARY_NAME = "vid2sub.glossary.toml"
SIDECAR_SUFFIX = ".vid2sub.jsonl"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class TrackInfo:
    key: str
    lang: str
    script: str | None
    file_code: str


TRACKS: dict[str, TrackInfo] = {
    t.key: t
    for t in (
        TrackInfo("en", "en", None, "en"),
        TrackInfo("ja", "ja", None, "ja"),
        TrackInfo("ko", "ko", None, "ko"),
        TrackInfo("zh-Hans", "zh", "Hans", "zh-CN"),
        TrackInfo("zh-Hant", "zh", "Hant", "zh-TW"),
        TrackInfo("yue-Hant", "yue", "Hant", "zh-HK"),
    )
}
_ALIASES = {"zh": "zh-Hans", "yue": "yue-Hant", "eng": "en", "jpn": "ja", "kor": "ko",
            "chi": "zh-Hans", "zho": "zh-Hans"}


class UnknownTrack(ValueError):
    pass


def track(key: str) -> TrackInfo:
    try:
        return TRACKS[key]
    except KeyError:
        raise UnknownTrack(f"unknown track {key!r}; known: {', '.join(TRACKS)}") from None


def source_track(lang: str, script: str | None) -> str:
    if lang == "zh":
        return "zh-Hant" if script == "hant" else "zh-Hans"
    if lang == "yue":
        if script == "hans":
            raise UnknownTrack("Cantonese subtitles are Traditional only in this version")
        return "yue-Hant"
    return track(lang).key


def target_track(value: str) -> str:
    lowered = value.strip().lower()
    for key in TRACKS:
        if key.lower() == lowered:
            return key
    if lowered in _ALIASES:
        return _ALIASES[lowered]
    raise UnknownTrack(f"unknown target {value!r}; known: {', '.join(TRACKS)}")


def stem(video_name: str) -> str:
    return PurePath(video_name).stem


def subtitle_name(video_name: str, track_key: str, fmt: str, *,
                  sdh: bool = False, forced: bool = False) -> str:
    parts = [stem(video_name), track(track_key).file_code]
    if sdh:
        parts.append("sdh")
    if forced:
        parts.append("forced")
    return ".".join(parts) + f".{fmt}"


def sidecar_name(video_name: str) -> str:
    return stem(video_name) + SIDECAR_SUFFIX


def track_from_subtitle_name(video_name: str, sub_name: str) -> str | None:
    base = stem(video_name) + "."
    sub_stem = PurePath(sub_name).stem
    if not sub_stem.startswith(base):
        return None
    code = sub_stem[len(base):].split(".")[0].lower()
    for info in TRACKS.values():
        if info.file_code.lower() == code or info.key.lower() == code:
            return info.key
    return _ALIASES.get(code)
```

```markdown
# Contracts with recordings

vid2sub's transcript renditions use recordings' `recordings/rendition@1` envelope and its
`TranscriptPayload` exactly (spec §14.3), so recordings can import them. These schemas are copies
from recordings at commit `64b75513876207f6e4d0ddfd7191497551c6e200`. When recordings changes its
rendition format, copy the new schema here (with its commit) and re-run `tests/test_rendition.py`.
```

```python
import pytest

from vid2sub.subs.sidecar import (
    Conflict, Header, HeadRecord, IdentityRecord, ProjectionRecord, Sidecar, SidecarError,
    VideoInfo, make_rendition, parse_sidecar, serialize,
)

HEADER = Header(video=VideoInfo(name="Show.mkv", size=10, quick_sha256="ab", duration_ms=1000))
AT = "2026-10-14T12:00:00Z"


def head(track, rid, replaces=None, by="auto"):
    return HeadRecord(track=track, rendition=rid, replaces=replaces, by=by, at=AT)


def test_a_rendition_id_is_a_hash_of_its_content():
    a = make_rendition([], "en", {"x": 1})
    assert a.id.startswith("r_") and len(a.id) == 18
    assert make_rendition([], "en", {"x": 1}).id == a.id
    assert make_rendition([], "en", {"x": 2}).id != a.id
    assert make_rendition([a.id], "en", {"x": 1}).id != a.id


def test_a_sidecar_round_trips_through_jsonl():
    r = make_rendition([], "en", {"kind": "cues"})
    side = Sidecar(HEADER).add(r, head("en", r.id), IdentityRecord(sha256="ff", at=AT))
    text = serialize(side)
    assert text.endswith("\n") and text.count("\n") == 4
    again, warnings = parse_sidecar(text)
    assert again == side and warnings == []


def test_a_truncated_last_line_is_ignored_and_reported():
    r = make_rendition([], "en", {"kind": "cues"})
    text = serialize(Sidecar(HEADER).add(r)) + '{"type":"head","tra'
    side, warnings = parse_sidecar(text)
    assert list(side.renditions()) == [r.id]
    assert warnings == ["ignored a truncated last line"]


def test_a_broken_line_in_the_middle_refuses_the_whole_file():
    r = make_rendition([], "en", {"kind": "cues"})
    lines = serialize(Sidecar(HEADER).add(r)).splitlines()
    text = "\n".join([lines[0], "not json", lines[1]]) + "\n"
    with pytest.raises(SidecarError, match="line 2"):
        parse_sidecar(text)


def test_heads_follow_replacements_and_a_divergence_is_a_conflict():
    r1, r2, r3 = (make_rendition([], "en", {"n": i}) for i in range(3))
    side = Sidecar(HEADER).add(r1, r2, r3, head("en", r1.id), head("en", r2.id, r1.id, "editor"))
    assert side.head("en").rendition == r2.id
    diverged = side.add(head("en", r3.id, r1.id))
    result = diverged.head("en")
    assert isinstance(result, Conflict) and set(result.leaves) == {r2.id, r3.id}
    assert side.head("ja") is None


def test_adding_the_same_record_twice_keeps_one():
    r = make_rendition([], "en", {"n": 1})
    side = Sidecar(HEADER).add(r).add(r)
    assert len(side.records) == 1


def test_the_latest_projection_per_file_wins():
    p1 = ProjectionRecord(path="Show.en.srt", format="srt", track="en", rendition="r_1",
                          sha256="a", at=AT)
    p2 = p1.model_copy(update={"sha256": "b", "rendition": "r_2"})
    assert Sidecar(HEADER).add(p1, p2).projections()["Show.en.srt"].sha256 == "b"
```

`tests/test_rendition.py`:
```python
import json
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path

from jsonschema import Draft202012Validator

from vid2sub.subs.model import Cue, Segment, Transcript, Word
from vid2sub.subs.rendition import (
    cues_body, cues_from_body, transcript_body, transcript_from_body,
)

CONTRACTS = Path(__file__).parent / "contracts"
T = Transcript(language="ja", language_probs={"ja": 0.97}, segments=(
    Segment(text="こんにちは", start_ms=1000, end_ms=2000, avg_logprob=-0.2,
            words=(Word(text="こん", start_ms=1000, end_ms=1500, prob=0.9),
                   Word(text="にちは", start_ms=1500, end_ms=1500, timed=False))),
    Segment(text="lost timings", start_ms=3000, end_ms=4000),
))


def body():
    return transcript_body(T, engine="whisper.cpp", model="whisper-large-v3-turbo",
                           version="whisper-large-v3-turbo@unknown+v1234abcd+p00000000",
                           created_at="2026-10-14T12:00:00Z",
                           inputs={"audio_sha256": "aa", "audio_track": 1}, meta={"vad": "x"})


def test_a_transcript_body_is_a_valid_recordings_rendition():
    b = body()
    Draft202012Validator(json.loads((CONTRACTS / "recordings-rendition.schema.json").read_text())
                         ).validate(b)
    Draft202012Validator(json.loads(
        (CONTRACTS / "recordings-transcript-payload.schema.json").read_text())
    ).validate(b["payload"])
    assert datetime.fromisoformat(b["created_at"].replace("Z", "+00:00")).tzinfo is not None
    assert b["inputs"]["audio_track"] == "1"
    assert b["payload"]["segments"][0]["start"] == 1.0


def test_a_transcript_body_reads_back_with_word_probabilities_and_untimed_words():
    assert transcript_from_body(body()) == T


def test_a_cues_body_round_trips():
    cues = [Cue(id="c1", start_ms=0, end_ms=900, text="Hi", w=(0, 1)),
            Cue(id="c2", start_ms=1000, end_ms=1900, text="There", src=("s1", "s2"), src_sha="x")]
    b = cues_body(cues, language="en", script=None, engine="vid2sub", model=None,
                  version="cues@1", created_at="2026-10-14T12:00:00Z", inputs={}, meta={},
                  glossary_sha="g1")
    assert b["schema"] == "vid2sub/cues@1" and b["payload"]["glossary_sha"] == "g1"
    assert cues_from_body(b) == cues
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement `sidecar.py`**

`src/vid2sub/subs/sidecar.py`:
```python
"""The sidecar next to a video: an append-only JSON Lines file (spec §14).

Line 1 is a header; every other line is one immutable record. A merge of two writers is the union
of their records, so nothing is ever lost. Rendition ids are hashes of their content. Each track's
current version is named by head records; a head record says which head it replaces, so the
current head is the one nothing replaced, and two of those mean two writers diverged: a conflict
the user resolves (plan decision 4). A truncated last line (a crash mid-write) is ignored and
reported; any other unreadable line stops everything, because vid2sub never overwrites a sidecar
it can't read (spec §14.6). Paths are basenames only.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import json
from collections.abc import Sequence
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Annotated, Any, Literal

from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field, TypeAdapter, ValidationError

SIDECAR_SCHEMA = "vid2sub/sidecar@1"
HeadBy = Literal["auto", "editor", "external", "import", "user"]


class _Rec(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(frozen=True, extra="forbid", populate_by_name=True)


class VideoInfo(_Rec):
    name: str
    size: int
    quick_sha256: str
    duration_ms: int


class Header(_Rec):
    schema_: Literal["vid2sub/sidecar@1"] = Field(default=SIDECAR_SCHEMA, alias="schema")
    video: VideoInfo


class IdentityRecord(_Rec):
    type: Literal["identity"] = "identity"
    sha256: str
    at: str


class RenditionRecord(_Rec):
    type: Literal["rendition"] = "rendition"
    id: str
    parents: tuple[str, ...] = ()
    track: str | None
    body: dict[str, Any]


class HeadRecord(_Rec):
    type: Literal["head"] = "head"
    track: str
    rendition: str
    replaces: str | None = None
    by: HeadBy
    at: str


class ProjectionRecord(_Rec):
    type: Literal["projection"] = "projection"
    path: str
    format: Literal["srt", "ass"]
    track: str
    rendition: str
    sha256: str
    at: str


Record = Annotated[IdentityRecord | RenditionRecord | HeadRecord | ProjectionRecord,
                   Field(discriminator="type")]
_RECORD: TypeAdapter[Record] = TypeAdapter(Record)


class SidecarError(ValueError):
    pass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Conflict:
    track: str
    leaves: tuple[str, ...]


def canonical_json(obj: Any) -> str:
    return json.dumps(obj, ensure_ascii=False, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"))


def _dump(rec: BaseModel) -> dict[str, Any]:
    return rec.model_dump(mode="json", by_alias=True)


def make_rendition(parents: Sequence[str], track: str | None,
                   body: dict[str, Any]) -> RenditionRecord:
    content = {"type": "rendition", "parents": list(parents), "track": track, "body": body}
    rid = "r_" + hashlib.sha256(canonical_json(content).encode()).hexdigest()[:16]
    return RenditionRecord(id=rid, parents=tuple(parents), track=track, body=body)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Sidecar:
    header: Header
    records: tuple[Any, ...] = field(default_factory=tuple)

    def add(self, *records: Any) -> Sidecar:
        seen = {canonical_json(_dump(r)) for r in self.records}
        new = list(self.records)
        for r in records:
            key = canonical_json(_dump(r))
            if key not in seen:
                seen.add(key)
                new.append(r)
        return Sidecar(self.header, tuple(new))

    def renditions(self) -> dict[str, RenditionRecord]:
        return {r.id: r for r in self.records if isinstance(r, RenditionRecord)}

    def transcripts(self) -> list[RenditionRecord]:
        return [r for r in self.records
                if isinstance(r, RenditionRecord) and r.body.get("kind") == "transcript"]

    def heads(self, track: str) -> list[HeadRecord]:
        return [r for r in self.records if isinstance(r, HeadRecord) and r.track == track]

    def head(self, track: str) -> HeadRecord | Conflict | None:
        heads = self.heads(track)
        if not heads:
            return None
        replaced = {h.replaces for h in heads if h.replaces}
        leaves: dict[str, HeadRecord] = {}
        for h in heads:
            if h.rendition not in replaced:
                leaves[h.rendition] = h
        if not leaves:
            return heads[-1]
        if len(leaves) == 1:
            return next(iter(leaves.values()))
        return Conflict(track, tuple(leaves))

    def tracks(self) -> list[str]:
        out: list[str] = []
        for r in self.records:
            if isinstance(r, HeadRecord) and r.track not in out:
                out.append(r.track)
        return out

    def projections(self) -> dict[str, ProjectionRecord]:
        out: dict[str, ProjectionRecord] = {}
        for r in self.records:
            if isinstance(r, ProjectionRecord):
                out[r.path] = r
        return out

    def identity(self) -> str | None:
        found = [r.sha256 for r in self.records if isinstance(r, IdentityRecord)]
        return found[-1] if found else None


def parse_sidecar(text: str) -> tuple[Sidecar, list[str]]:
    warnings: list[str] = []
    lines = text.split("\n")
    if lines and lines[-1] == "":
        lines.pop()
    elif lines:
        try:
            json.loads(lines[-1])
        except ValueError:
            warnings.append("ignored a truncated last line")
            lines.pop()
    if not lines:
        raise SidecarError("the sidecar is empty")
    try:
        header = Header.model_validate(json.loads(lines[0]))
    except (ValueError, ValidationError):
        raise SidecarError("line 1 isn't a vid2sub sidecar header; refusing to write over it") \
            from None
    records = []
    for number, line in enumerate(lines[1:], start=2):
        if not line.strip():
            continue
        try:
            records.append(_RECORD.validate_python(json.loads(line)))
        except (ValueError, ValidationError):
            raise SidecarError(f"line {number} isn't a vid2sub record; refusing to write over a "
                               "sidecar vid2sub can't read") from None
    return Sidecar(header, tuple(records)), warnings


def serialize(sidecar: Sidecar) -> str:
    lines = [canonical_json(_dump(sidecar.header))]
    lines += [canonical_json(_dump(r)) for r in sidecar.records]
    return "\n".join(lines) + "\n"
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Implement `rendition.py`**

`src/vid2sub/subs/rendition.py`:
```python
"""Rendition bodies: transcripts in recordings' envelope, cue tracks in vid2sub's (spec §14.3).

A transcript body is byte-compatible with recordings' `recordings/rendition@1` and its
TranscriptPayload (seconds, no extra fields), so recordings can import it; word probabilities,
untimed words and per-segment statistics live in `meta`. `inputs` is `dict[str, str]`, so every
value is stringified. Cue tracks use `vid2sub/cues@1` with the same field names.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Sequence
from typing import Any

from vid2sub.subs.model import Cue, Segment, Transcript, Word

TRANSCRIPT_SCHEMA = "recordings/rendition@1"
CUES_SCHEMA = "vid2sub/cues@1"


def _s(ms: int) -> float:
    return round(ms / 1000, 3)


def _ms(seconds: float) -> int:
    return round(seconds * 1000)


def transcript_body(t: Transcript, *, engine: str, model: str | None, version: str,
                    created_at: str, inputs: dict[str, Any], meta: dict[str, Any]) -> dict:
    segments, probs, untimed, stats = [], [], [], []
    index = 0
    for seg in t.segments:
        words = []
        for w in seg.words:
            words.append({"word": w.text, "start": _s(w.start_ms), "end": _s(w.end_ms)})
            probs.append(w.prob)
            if not w.timed:
                untimed.append(index)
            index += 1
        segments.append({"start": _s(seg.start_ms), "end": _s(seg.end_ms), "speaker": None,
                         "speaker_name": None, "embedding_key": None, "text": seg.text,
                         "words": words})
        stats.append({"avg_logprob": seg.avg_logprob, "no_speech_prob": seg.no_speech_prob,
                      "compression_ratio": seg.compression_ratio})
    return {
        "schema": TRANSCRIPT_SCHEMA, "kind": "transcript", "engine": engine, "model": model,
        "version": version, "created_at": created_at,
        "inputs": {k: str(v) for k, v in inputs.items()},
        "meta": {**meta, "word_prob": probs, "untimed_words": untimed, "segment_stats": stats,
                 "language_probs": dict(t.language_probs)},
        "payload": {"language": t.language, "segments": segments},
    }


def transcript_from_body(body: dict) -> Transcript:
    meta = body.get("meta", {})
    probs = meta.get("word_prob", [])
    untimed = set(meta.get("untimed_words", []))
    stats = meta.get("segment_stats", [])
    segments = []
    index = 0
    for k, seg in enumerate(body["payload"]["segments"]):
        words = []
        for w in seg.get("words", []):
            words.append(Word(text=w["word"], start_ms=_ms(w["start"]), end_ms=_ms(w["end"]),
                              prob=probs[index] if index < len(probs) else None,
                              timed=index not in untimed))
            index += 1
        st = stats[k] if k < len(stats) else {}
        segments.append(Segment(text=seg["text"], start_ms=_ms(seg["start"]),
                                end_ms=_ms(seg["end"]), words=tuple(words), **st))
    return Transcript(language=body["payload"]["language"], segments=tuple(segments),
                      language_probs=meta.get("language_probs", {}))


def cues_body(cues: Sequence[Cue], *, language: str, script: str | None, engine: str,
              model: str | None, version: str, created_at: str, inputs: dict[str, Any],
              meta: dict[str, Any], glossary_sha: str | None = None) -> dict:
    payload: dict[str, Any] = {"language": language, "script": script,
                               "cues": [c.dump() for c in cues]}
    if glossary_sha:
        payload["glossary_sha"] = glossary_sha
    return {"schema": CUES_SCHEMA, "kind": "cues", "engine": engine, "model": model,
            "version": version, "created_at": created_at,
            "inputs": {k: str(v) for k, v in inputs.items()}, "meta": dict(meta),
            "payload": payload}


def cues_from_body(body: dict) -> list[Cue]:
    return [Cue.model_validate(c) for c in body["payload"]["cues"]]


def body_track_language(body: dict) -> tuple[str, str | None]:
    return body["payload"]["language"], body["payload"].get("script")
```

```python
import pytest

from vid2sub.subs.glossary import (
    EMPTY_GLOSSARY, GlossaryError, chunk_prompt, parse_glossary, prompt_sha, vocabulary_prompt,
)

TOML = '''
description = "Detective Conan, a Japanese anime"
[terms]
"工藤新一" = "Shinichi Kudo"
"毛利蘭" = "Ran Mouri"
'''


def test_a_glossary_file_reads_its_description_and_term_pairs():
    g = parse_glossary(TOML)
    assert g.description == "Detective Conan, a Japanese anime"
    assert g.terms == (("工藤新一", "Shinichi Kudo"), ("毛利蘭", "Ran Mouri"))
    assert g.source_terms() == ["工藤新一", "毛利蘭"]


def test_the_glossary_hash_is_stable_and_empty_for_no_glossary():
    assert parse_glossary(TOML).sha() == parse_glossary(TOML).sha()
    assert len(parse_glossary(TOML).sha()) == 8
    assert EMPTY_GLOSSARY.sha() == ""
    assert parse_glossary(TOML.replace("Ran", "Rachel")).sha() != parse_glossary(TOML).sha()


@pytest.mark.parametrize("text, message", [
    ("nonsense = [", "isn't valid TOML"),
    ('colour = "x"', "unknown glossary key"),
    ("[terms]\na = 1", "pairs"),
])
def test_a_bad_glossary_says_what_is_wrong(text, message):
    with pytest.raises(GlossaryError, match=message):
        parse_glossary(text)


def test_the_vocabulary_prompt_is_a_plain_list_not_instructions():
    assert vocabulary_prompt(["Quarto", " pixi ", ""]) == "Quarto, pixi."
    assert vocabulary_prompt([]) == ""
    assert prompt_sha("") == "" and len(prompt_sha("a")) == 8


def test_a_chunk_prompt_puts_the_glossary_first_then_the_previous_words():
    g = parse_glossary(TOML)
    prompt = chunk_prompt(g, "and then he said hello", cjk=False)
    assert prompt.startswith("工藤新一, 毛利蘭.")
    assert prompt.endswith("and then he said hello")


def test_a_chunk_prompt_stays_within_its_budget():
    g = parse_glossary("[terms]\n" + "\n".join(f'"term{i}" = "t"' for i in range(200)))
    assert len(chunk_prompt(g, "x" * 1000, cjk=True)) <= 200
    assert len(chunk_prompt(EMPTY_GLOSSARY, "y" * 1000, cjk=False)) == 200
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`src/vid2sub/subs/glossary.py`:
```python
"""A show's glossary: names and terms with their preferred translations (spec §16).

`vid2sub.glossary.toml` sits in the show's folder. Its source terms become Whisper's prompt (a
comma-joined list, never instructions, an audio-router lesson) or Qwen3-ASR's context; its
description and pairs go into the translator's system prompt; its hash goes into translation
versions so a glossary edit marks translations stale. Whisper's prompt window is 224 tokens, so a
chunk prompt keeps a character budget: the glossary first, then the previous chunk's last words.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import json
import tomllib
from collections.abc import Iterable
from dataclasses import dataclass


class GlossaryError(ValueError):
    pass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Glossary:
    description: str = ""
    terms: tuple[tuple[str, str], ...] = ()

    @property
    def empty(self) -> bool:
        return not self.description and not self.terms

    def sha(self) -> str:
        if self.empty:
            return ""
        blob = json.dumps({"description": self.description,
                           "terms": [list(t) for t in self.terms]},
                          ensure_ascii=False, sort_keys=True)
        return hashlib.sha256(blob.encode()).hexdigest()[:8]

    def source_terms(self) -> list[str]:
        return [source for source, _ in self.terms]


EMPTY_GLOSSARY = Glossary()


def parse_glossary(text: str) -> Glossary:
    try:
        data = tomllib.loads(text)
    except tomllib.TOMLDecodeError as err:
        raise GlossaryError(f"the glossary isn't valid TOML: {err}") from None
    unknown = set(data) - {"description", "terms"}
    if unknown:
        raise GlossaryError(f"unknown glossary key(s): {', '.join(sorted(unknown))}")
    description = data.get("description", "")
    terms = data.get("terms", {})
    if (not isinstance(description, str) or not isinstance(terms, dict)
            or not all(isinstance(k, str) and isinstance(v, str) for k, v in terms.items())):
        raise GlossaryError('the glossary needs `description` as text and `[terms]` as '
                            '"source" = "translation" pairs')
    return Glossary(description.strip(),
                    tuple((k.strip(), v.strip()) for k, v in terms.items() if k.strip()))


# vocabulary_prompt and prompt_sha are copied from audio-router,
# src/audio_router/archive/stages/transcribe.py at 64612df.
def vocabulary_prompt(terms: Iterable[str] | None) -> str:
    """Whisper's `initial_prompt` as a plain sentence.

    Whisper conditions on it as if it were preceding transcript text, so a comma-joined list of
    the exact spellings is enough; phrasing it as instructions is how the model starts
    transcribing the instructions."""
    cleaned = [t.strip() for t in (terms or []) if t and t.strip()]
    return ", ".join(cleaned) + "." if cleaned else ""


def prompt_sha(prompt: str) -> str:
    return hashlib.sha256(prompt.encode()).hexdigest()[:8] if prompt else ""


def chunk_prompt(glossary: Glossary, previous_tail: str, *, cjk: bool) -> str:
    budget = 200 if cjk else 600
    vocab = vocabulary_prompt(glossary.source_terms())[:budget]
    room = budget - len(vocab) - (1 if vocab else 0)
    tail = previous_tail.strip()
    tail = tail[-min(200, room):] if room > 0 and tail else ""
    return f"{vocab} {tail}" if vocab and tail else (vocab or tail)
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`src/vid2sub/subs/scripts.py`:
```python
"""Traditional and Simplified Chinese, converted with OpenCC (spec §11.3).

Whisper writes Chinese in either script, so source tracks are converted to the track's script
after cues are built: on cue text, never on words, because conversion can change text length.
opencc-purepy is pure Python (it runs in Pyodide); it sits behind this module so it can be
swapped (council, 2026-10-14: it has one maintainer).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Sequence
from functools import lru_cache

from vid2sub.subs.model import Cue

CONFIG_FOR_TRACK = {"zh-Hans": "t2s", "zh-Hant": "s2tw", "yue-Hant": "s2hk"}


@lru_cache(maxsize=None)
def _converter(config: str):
    from opencc_purepy import OpenCC

    return OpenCC(config)


def config_for(track_key: str) -> str | None:
    return CONFIG_FOR_TRACK.get(track_key)


def convert(text: str, config: str) -> str:
    return _converter(config).convert(text)


def convert_cues(cues: Sequence[Cue], config: str) -> list[Cue]:
    return [c.model_copy(update={"text": convert(c.text, config)}) for c in cues]
```

### Task 12: The shape of translation

**Files:**
- Create: `src/vid2sub/subs/translate_shape.py`

**Interfaces:**

  strip_line_ends` (Task 3); `naming.track` (Task 8); `langs` (Task 2).
- Produces: `Batch(ids, before, after)`;
  `plan_batches(all_ids, selected=None, *, size=40, context=5) -> list[Batch]`;
  `split_batch(batch, all_ids, *, context=5) -> list[Batch]`;
  `batch_model(ids) -> type[BaseModel]` (fields: `items: list[{src: list[Literal[ids]], text: str}]`);
  `Item(src: tuple[str, ...], text: str)`; `items_from(result) -> list[Item]`;
  `PartitionError`; `validate_partition(items, ids) -> list[Item]`;
  `item_limits(cues, target_track) -> dict[str, int]`; `src_sha(texts) -> str`;
  `assemble(items, source: Mapping[str, Cue], target_track, taken) -> list[Cue]`;
  `stale_ids(translation, source) -> set[str]`; `untranslated(source_ids, translation) -> list[str]`.

`tests/test_translate_shape.py`:
